# ⟲ Wave-22 addendum (2026-07-23, doc-curator `w20-w22-sync`) — **the performance wave: the Head reset the scoring, the primitive claim survived its sharpest test, and exactly one edge survived**

> **⚠ Provenance:** Hub §10 entry 2026-07-23 night ("REVIEWED 3/3 + INTEGRATED → main GREEN 584"); no Hub ledger addendum. Three engineer reports, all merged: `designed-mechanism-learned-content`, `gated-write-performance`, `hopfield-capacity-benchmark`.

## ⭐ THE HEAD RESET THE SCORING (binding framing — adopt in every chapter's reading, recorded verbatim)
- **PERFORMANCE IS THE DELIVERABLE, not theory:** *"if the property theoretically fails but we still get good task-performance that's still a massive win… Nobody knows why transformers work… grounding from physical laws is ideal but not essential."* ⇒ **the score is: how many external benchmarks does CLU win on their own headline metric? Currently ZERO.**
- **FILING RULE (binding):** property-demo without performance → **workshop shorts**; performance without explanation → **ICLR**; performance WITH the explaining law → **ICLR/NMI**. **Do NOT discard property demos — file them by how much performance backs them.**
- **DO NOT pre-register the retreat:** *"We will keep finding novel ways of reaching our primitive goal… we are completely reimagining things to see if things previously thought impossible can work."* And: *"the more benchmarks you beat the more liked you are."*
- ⚠ **Ledger note:** this rescoring changes how every chapter's verdict should be *read* (a validated mechanism with no performance now files as a short), but it changes no *measurement*. The scorecard below tags rows with their filing tier where the Head's rule bites.

## Chapter-by-chapter deltas

**Ch-8 candidate — ⭐ THE PRIMITIVE-DECIDER: the K=8 wall is GEOMETRY, not learning; the primitive claim is ALIVE; the exponent is unpinned.**
- `designed-mechanism-learned-content` §1: clean curve **K_learned = 4, 8, 8, 32, 8 at d=2,3,4,6,8**. **H-LEARNING REJECTED** (d=6 clears 32, 8× the d=2 wall; d=2's own K=8 genuinely fails at 0.768 with adequate budget) — w21's "sharpest open question" answered; `potential-function-class`'s "atoms break at K=8" was the d=2 point of a rising curve. **NOT a clean exponential** (5-pt base 1.18, R²0.26; d≤6 base 1.64, R²0.94). **The d=8=8 point is an OPTIMIZER FAILURE, near-provably** (4× more geometric room than d=6; capacity cannot shrink with room; writer stalled on trivially-separated cells, rescued 0.40→1.00 by budget). Corrected reading: **4→8→~16→32, a real rising trend**; **the budget must scale with address DIMENSION** — the dimension-aware re-run (`min_atoms ∝ c^d`) is the owed follow-up that converts "probably geometry" into "geometry, proven." Learned pays a **widening tax** vs designed (¼→⅛→≤1/16). **N82.**
- **Mass stores nothing, 3rd confirmation** (coupling 0.081<0.1; per-item mass Δstrict −0.005±0.007 — a *pre-registered confirmation* of Prop F1 in a new regime). ⭐ **The write operator AMPLIFIES with d: 197× (d=2) → 599× (d=3) → 6332× (d=4)** masked-vs-global — the program's most robust mechanistic result strengthens in higher dimension.

**Ch-8 candidate (cont.) — the gated-write performance test: LEVELLING, not beating; ONE edge survives.**
- `gated-write-performance`: gated CLU wins **0 of 3 absolute** — the gate imports a conjunction capability every baseline already has (adding: 79× off the floor, still 3.3× worse than GRU; parity ~chance — a gate is a conjunction, not running-XOR state; MQAR ties GRU 0.4993 vs 0.4863, far below attention 0.995).
- ⭐⭐ **THE ONE SURVIVING EDGE — capacity under item load:** gated CLU at γ=0 **crosses above a matched, rescued, gated GRU at kv≈4; 1.7× at kv=8; 2.1× at kv=16 (0.180 vs 0.085)**. Survives matched gating AND tuning; the property **pre-exists the gate** (linear CLU crosses at kv≈8) ⇒ **a CLU architectural property, not a gate artifact** — the strongest CLU-favourable performance signal, under the strictest fairness. ⛔ **CORRECTION PROGRAM-WIDE: 19×/17× → 2.1× (N78).** ⚠ Attention still wins every cell. Cost of the edge: **1.48× GRU wall-clock / 2.16× FLOPs**.
- ⛔ **Two falsifications that fence the physics story (N79, N80):** *"symplectic ⇒ stable extrapolation"* fails in the trained driven slot — gated CLU is the **WORST** of three (in-dist 0.0020 → 2.01 at 2× → 2.96 at 4×, worse than predicting the mean) ⇒ **scope "CLU extrapolates" to the autonomous/designed setting ONLY**; and **Prop-2 robustness does not transfer** (worse at every noise level, steeper slope +0.406 vs +0.242).

**Ch. 4 (test-time compute) — ⭐ THE RETRY FINALLY HAS A MEASURED BENEFIT, and it is the program's most promising thread.** `hopfield-capacity-benchmark` §4.1: a second boosted relaxation on the bottom-50%-confidence queries → **+46.9pp (0.492→0.961) at ×1.5 compute, with a −38.3pp blank guard** (retrying the confident half destroys it) — the exact adaptive-compute signature, **a curve a single-softmax-step memory cannot draw**. Registered +2–8pp, under-predicted ~7×. The w19 promotion (*"structural role, still unmeasured"*) is now **measured**; the w19 novelty verdict (*"a mechanism in search of a demonstrated benefit"*) is **half-discharged** — the benefit exists, the external benchmark does not. Files per the Head's rule as a **short now, ICLR the moment it carries a benchmark**; it sits squarely in the field's hottest area. Companion capability: **per-item retention** (permanent + scheduled-decay items in one store, no Hopfield time axis).

**Ch-8 candidate (cont.) — the benchmark verdict: we picked a memory benchmark that doesn't need memory.** ⛔ A **trivial NN pixel floor beats everyone** (CLU and modern Hopfield; MNIST ≥0.996 to M=512) — the visible half names the image. CLU ties/loses closed-form Hopfield on capacity; ⭐ **wins the noise axis vs Hopfield** (MNIST σ=0.2: 0.92 vs 0.42; CIFAR σ≤0.2: 0.86 vs 0.008 — *rolling to the bottom of a valley IS denoising; one softmax step is not*) but still loses to NN. ⚠ **Admissibility caveat that travels: "nothing learned on both sides" is MNIST-ONLY** — closed-form Hopfield retrieves raw-pixel CIFAR **at chance for all β** (DC-dominated inner products), which is *why* U-Hop's SOTA learns a kernel. Protocol pins: success = mean **`sqdiff`** (not cosine>0.9); mask = `torch.dropout(0.5)` (zero+×2). **N81.**

**⭐ THE PLAIN-LANGUAGE RE-GROUNDING (Head-requested; the program had over-jargoned — carry this framing into every onboarding doc).** *CLU = a hilly landscape with a ball. Memory = the shape of the hills (each item a valley). Recall = drop the ball near where the answer should be, let it roll and settle, read where it stopped.* **Write** = shape a valley at a chosen spot (where · how deep/narrow · permanent-pit vs leaky-valley · the ball's mass). **Read** = query → start position, pick a mass, roll with friction, read the resting spot (+ shove-and-retry). ⛔ **The big gap, plainly: there is NO learned feature extractor (read-in φ) and NO learned read-out ψ — we are "running attention on raw pixels with no embedding layer."** The clever learned addressing is the thing that died (N61); today the query's own position is the start and the ball rolls to the nearest valley (content-addressable, like Hopfield); the retry trigger = how close the ball settled to a known valley. ⚠ Precision for any writeup: **a frozen surface does not decay on its own** — "leaky valley" decay is either a within-rollout fade (half-life in rollout steps) or an active edit (lowering well depth, as the retention demo did by hand).

# ⟲ Wave-23 addendum (2026-07-24, doc-curator `w23-sync`) — **we built the three missing pieces, and the same shape appeared three times: the baseline owns the headline, CLU owns the mechanism**

> **⚠ Provenance:** written from the six w23 output reports (`phi-read-in`, `retry-compute-study`, `controller-mvp`, `dimension-aware-budget`, `continual-learning-recon`, `r19-r20-reconciliations`) plus the Hub's review direction at the commissioning of this pass (wave-23 reviewed 6/6, integrated, `main @ 5e466c0`, **612 green**). **No Hub §10 wave-23 entry existed at the time of writing** — the live handover entry was still `2026-07-23 (SESSION CLOSE)`, which lists the wave as in-flight. ⚠ **Numbers here are transcribed from the reports; verdicts are not reinterpreted.** Where a report and the Hub's direction differ in emphasis, the report's number governs and the divergence is flagged (see doc-hygiene).

## ⭐⭐ THE CROSS-CUTTING FINDING OF THE WAVE (record it as the program's honest position, not as a coincidence)

**Across three independent tasks — the learned read-in `φ`, the retry compute curve, and the controller rematch — the SAME shape recurred: a trivial or strong baseline dominates the absolute headline number, while CLU owns a mechanism/capability axis that those baselines structurally lack.**

| task | the baseline that owns the headline | the axis CLU owns |
|---|---|---|
| **φ read-in** | **kNN-in-φ** — 1.000 to M=128; laundering fires on **all 4** cells, max CLU margin **0.000** (N89) | φ + settling dynamics: raw-CLU **chance 0.012 → 0.81–0.97** on CIFAR; **CLU-in-φ ≫ closed-form Hopfield-in-φ** at load/noise (CIFAR M=256: **0.973 vs 0.008**); the **retry confidence gate survives φ** (AUROC 0.845–0.988) |
| **retry curve** | **feedforward-NN**, flat at ceiling **0.99–1.00**, beats CLU-gated in **all 8** cells (−3.5…−42.2 pp) (N90) | a **rising, auto-stopping** accuracy-vs-compute curve (+6.6…+76.2 pp, ×1.2–1.8) whose mechanism **survives every control**: random-kick and ensemble-of-k are **dead flat**; the gate is load-bearing (ungated 0.96→0.004) |
| **controller** | **GRU 0.57** on retention-per-offered at fixed geometry (CLU 0.081, last of seven) (N91) | **per-admitted 1.000 flat to K=64, best of five**; abstention with a certificate; permanent + leaky wells in one store; and per-offered **0.669 > gru 0.57** once the space is **sized** |

**Three independent tasks, one pattern.** The honest reading — and the one that should govern every draft — is that **the program's contribution is currently mechanistic and capability-shaped, not leaderboard-shaped**, and that the axes CLU owns are precisely the ones a static, metric-native benchmark cannot score. ⚠ This is *not* a retreat (the Head's no-retreat clause stands): it is a statement about **which benchmark can express the claim**, and it converges exactly with the recon's independent finding that the winnable slot is *"best replay-free method + per-item control + a retry curve,"* not *"beats replay."*

## Chapter-by-chapter deltas

**Ch. 4 (boost / cascade / test-time compute) — ⭐ THE MECHANISM SURVIVES EVERY CONTROL; the dominance claim does not.** `retry-compute-study` promoted w22's single point into a curve with five pre-registered controls. **Survivors (registry-grade):** the ladder rises **+6.6 … +76.2 pp** and **auto-stops at ×1.2–1.8** (the low-confidence tail exhausts); **equal-energy random-kick and ensemble-of-k independent restarts are DEAD FLAT in all 8 cells** ⇒ *the lift is the **directed** symplectic re-launch — not stochastic restart, not "just k tries"*; the **confidence gate is load-bearing** (ungated retry-all **collapses 0.96 → 0.004** at 9× compute; τ-sweep optimum 0.99 at **0.961@1.64×** vs no-gate 0.828@1.81×) — the w22 −38pp blank guard is now a continuous curve. ⛔ **Falsified and retracted:** the agent's own P1/P5b (dominance) — **the feedforward-NN floor is at ceiling 0.99–1.00 and beats CLU-gated in every cell** (N90). ⇒ **CM-23(b) splits: the *shape* claim survives (a saturated feedforward memory cannot rise), the *absolute-dominance* claim is dead.** ◐ Protocol-dependence is itself a finding: the boost recovers **masked** queries strongly and **Gaussian-noise** queries weakly, and past σ≈0.4 no retry can recover a query that has left every basin. New asset: the **RUD-C created-benchmark spec** (anchored verbatim to U-Hop `@cdac754`; four **mandatory** baselines; appendix-ready). Design note recorded so nobody re-derives it: **cosine-to-nearest-well is a ranking signal, never an acceptance signal** post-settle.

**Ch-8 candidate — ⭐⭐ THE CAPACITY LAW IS PINNED, and the ceiling belongs to the WRITE OPERATOR.** `dimension-aware-budget`: with a budget that scales with the address **dimension** (`n_atoms ≥ 512·(√2)^d`) plus per-point 2×-budget adequacy re-checks and a d=4 saturation curve over a **16× atom range**, the law is **`K_learned(d) = min(2^d, K_ceiling ≈ 32)`** — budget-adequate walls **{4, 8, 16, 32, 32, 32}** at d={2,3,4,5,6,8}. **For d ≤ 5 capacity doubles per dimension — EXACTLY the designed `4·2^d` rate ⇒ "the wall is geometry" is VINDICATED there.** For d ≥ 5 a **d-INDEPENDENT write ceiling** binds: **K=64 is unwritable at d=6 AND d=8 despite 4× different geometric room**, it is not geometry (designed ≥256) and **not parameters** (d=6 K=64 gets *worse* at 2× atoms, 0.855→0.809). ⇒ **the w22 d=8 stall was budget starvation (8→32, monotonicity restored)**, **H-LEARNING-at-8 is rejected**, and the designed/learned tax **stops widening** (plateaus ~1/8 for d≥5). ⛔ **Superseded:** N82's "exponent unpinned, base 1.2–1.7" *and* the temptation to quote the raw-sweep **base √2 (1.44) / `d^1.62`** — that fit averages two regimes (N92). ⚠ Prop F1's **fourth** mass null lands here too (coupling 0.145±0.085; per-item mass Δstrict **+0.008±0.011**), and masked-write locality reconfirms at **8474× (d=2) / 3434× (d=4)**.

**Ch-8 candidate (cont.) — ⭐ THE DOCTRINE FLAGSHIP: the premise is validated and the hoped-for consequence is falsified.** `phi-read-in` built a real learned `φ` in two independently-fair arms (frozen PCA-32; a separately-trained AE — both fit on a **disjoint** pool, **never through the store**, reconstruction loss only) around a **designed** store. **Premise validated (large):** on CIFAR, raw-space CLU is at **chance (0.012)** and **CLU-in-φ reaches 0.81–0.97**; closed-form Hopfield-in-φ also recovers from raw chance; and **CLU-in-φ ≫ closed-form Hopfield-in-φ** at load and noise (**CIFAR M=256: 0.973 vs 0.008**; MNIST σ=0.4: 0.852 vs 0.250) — *rolling to the bottom of a valley in feature space is a real, large lift over both the raw-pixel line and a one-step softmax read.* ⛔ **And the central optimistic hypothesis is falsified: the LAUNDERING CONTROL FIRES ON ALL FOUR (dataset × arm) CELLS — max CLU margin 0.000.** *"The win is φ's, not ours."* **This closes the "it only loses because there's no embedding" escape hatch** — the embedding now exists, twice, and the designed store still does not beat a trivial kNN over the same features (N89). ⚠ Scope the CLU>Hopfield margin: it is a **CIFAR / hard-feature-geometry** effect — on MNIST at high M Hopfield is slightly ahead (0.957 vs 0.871). ⚠ And the well-width rule `s = 0.3·median-NN` puts the store **exactly at the packing bound** (slack ≡ 1.08, *no headroom*), which is mechanically why CLU-in-φ decays before kNN as M grows.

**Ch-8 candidate (cont.) — ⭐ THE CONTROLLER EXISTS, and its verdict is two-part by construction.** `controller-mvp` built MVC-0 hand-coded, no learning: **admission** (spacing gate + refuse-and-relocate — and on this geometry **the gate demonstrably fires**: live-site spacing **1.61 ≥ d_safe 1.54**, intervention **0.20 → 0.97**, in direct contrast to N74's arithmetically-vacuous gate), **placement** (derived address, nothing searched — Prop 5's dead gradient routed around), **eviction** (LRU/depth; permanent never evicted; a full all-permanent store raises a **capacity alarm** instead of silently overwriting), **decay** (leaky wells, amplitude exactly `exp(−0.35t)`, half-life 1.98, `decayed_out = 6/6`, permanent untouched). **N75 rematch:** controller-OFF reproduces w21 **exactly** (0.110 at K=16 = designed_ungated) and then dies (0.000 at K=64); controller-ON is **per-admitted 1.000 flat to K=64 — best of five**; per-offered is **geometry-conditional — 0.081 (fixed, LAST of seven, below even the learned CLU it replaces) but 0.669 (sized, BEATS all four primitives, gru 0.57)**. ⇒ **the binding constraint is the address space, not the controller** — and **a single-number rematch claim is false** (N91). Two honest refinements (N93): sized geometry stops at **0.669** because random disk proposals achieve **42.8/64** packing (`d_eff<d`), and the **gate is cheap (0.006 ms) while refuse-and-relocate is ~4× a read (3.1 ms vs 0.70 ms)**.

**Ch. 6 / Ch. 7 (sampler thermodynamics; training dynamics) — ⭐ A NEW STANDING METHODOLOGICAL CAVEAT, earned by two dissolved conflicts.** `r19-r20-reconciliations` adjudicated all three open measurement conflicts, and **none was a physics disagreement or a bug** — they were **launch-convention and training-maturity differences**. **(i) R19-1:** the sign of the persistence result is carried **entirely by the rollout launch frame**; flipping L-2 → L-1 with everything else fixed flips 0.4568 (win) → **0.7705 (loss)** against persistence 0.5669, reproducing both original numbers; **γ is exactly neutral at n=1**. ⇒ **N86's conclusion is UPHELD and UNBLOCKED** (quote the L-1 number); the audit's **0.4545 "win" is retired** as a stale-launch artifact — a first-order extrapolator scored one cycle early. **(ii) R20-1:** **98.3% ballistic was a 40-epoch/2000-window under-trained fit** — the trained figure is **76.97% legacy / 50.49% shipped `dt`**; carrier = **epochs (−10 pp)**, not `dt`. **(iii) R19-2:** the reinstated single-basin collapse **does not reproduce** — it is a **~10-epoch transient** (spread **9.15e-08 at ep10 → 0.31–0.67 at ep150**, 100% finite, f32≡f64), it is **neither overflow nor a trained-model property**, the shipped encode path does **not** collapse (spread 0.456), and it **must not be promoted to an N-entry**; only the milder observation survives — *the trained `q*` cloud is low-rank (participation ratio ≈1, a near-1-D settled ray)*. ⇒ **new standing caveat, registered as N94: diagnostics computed on `<40`-epoch CAFE fits are not properties of the shipped model; every CAFE diagnostic states its epoch count.** This joins the w13 "instrument validity precedes inference" family and the w17 overflow rule.

**Ch. 7 (epistemics) — the prereg discipline had its best wave yet, on both signs.** Four pre-registrations were written before their harnesses ran, and **every one of the four central optimistic hypotheses was reported dead by the agent who registered it**: φ's "the designed store beats the trivial feature baseline" (laundered, 4/4), retry's P1/P5b dominance (NN floor above in 8/8), the controller's P5 (*the agent registered its own headline loss in advance and confirmed it*: 0.081 < 0.57), and the budget re-run's registered exponent band [1.55, 1.75] (the truth is base 2 **and** a ceiling — an unregistered ⚑ finding the agent flagged as *"the real result"*). Two prereg items were **refined rather than confirmed** and reported as such (controller P7 0.669-not-1.0; P9 relocation ≈4× a read). ⭐ **And a declared deviation done correctly:** `dimension-aware-budget` registered `c=2.0`, measured thinning ≈√2/dim in a probe, **declared the change to `c=√2` and showed the K-predictions were unaffected** — the deviation is in the scorecard, not buried.

## Gap-list updates

- **The score on the Head's metric is unchanged: 0 external benchmarks won on their own headline metric.** Wave-23 did not attempt one; it built the instruments (φ, the retry curve + RUD-C spec, MVC-0) and picked the target (rehearsal-free Class-IL).
- **⭐ The next experiment the wave itself names as highest-value: does a masked / curriculum / sequential write break the d-independent `K_ceiling ≈ 32`?** The ceiling is provably not geometry and not parameter count; masked writes are bit-local at any `d` (8474×/3434×). This is the cheapest path to converting the capacity law from `min(2^d, 32)` into `2^d` over a useful range.
- **⭐ The φ-space retry extension is GREEN and unattempted** (confidence AUROC 0.845–0.988 in φ-space) — and it is the closest available instance of *"a query the store is not metric-native to,"* which is exactly the regime where the NN floor should stop being at ceiling.
- **The CL entry is scoped but not built, and its scope caveat is binding:** *Class-IL at small scale is solved by replay (DGR 90.79% / iCaRL 94.57% vs EWC/SI ≈20%) and by a dumb balanced buffer (GDumb) — the winnable claim is "best replay-FREE method + per-item retention control + an adaptive-compute retry read," never "beats replay."* **Mandatory baselines: tuned ER + iCaRL + GDumb at matched memory + EWC/SI as the known null.** SQHN (Nature Comms 2024) occupies the neighbouring slot and must be cited and differentiated (continuous landscape · fiber payload · per-item decay · retry · learned φ).
- **A design rule now travels with any bounded-store benchmark:** size the address space to the load (packing bound ≥ K) **or** accept abstention and report per-admitted **with the admitted fraction**. Betting on per-offered without sizing reproduces the GRU loss.
- 🔴 **Superseded numbers/claims named (⟲, this wave):** **98.3% ballistic → ≈77–80% legacy / ≈50% shipped `dt`** (under-trained artifact) · the audit's **0.4545 persistence "win" → retired** (L-2 stale-launch artifact; the quotable number is **0.7705 vs 0.5669**, CLU loses) · **"the learned potential has one basin" / spread 0.0000 → retired** (a ~10-epoch transient; keep only "trained `q*` is low-rank, PR≈1") · **the w22 learned-capacity curve {4,8,8,32,8} → {4,8,16,32,32,32}** and **N82's "unpinned exponent, base 1.2–1.7" → `min(2^d, ~32)`** · **base √2 (1.44) / `d^1.62` → never quote as the exponent** · **CM-23's "an accuracy-vs-compute curve feedforward memories cannot draw" → SHAPE ONLY** (absolute dominance falsified, −3.5…−42.2 pp) · **"it only loses because there is no embedding" → closed** (N89) · **"CLU+controller wins/loses the rematch" as a single number → false** (both metrics travel).
- **Still owed (inventory, unchanged unless noted):** the **Chapter-8 decision** (now **four** waves overdue) · Hub confirmation of the **N89–N94 tiers** and of **N86's final tier** post-unblocking · claims-matrix **v2.2** (the CM-23 split + the two new forbidden claims; curator-proposed this pass) · voraus re-run post-`dt` · a real Mamba baseline · multi-seed error bars across the whole w23 frontier · R19-3 / R19-4 (untouched by design; R19-4 remains a *proposal under test*, and the Head's phase doctrine "learn around a designed core" is its working resolution, not its settlement).

# ⟲ Wave-24 addendum, part 1 of 2 — the DOCTRINE + STRUCTURE half (2026-07-24, doc-curator `w24-chapter-8`) — **the thesis changed, and the thing that changed it was a control we ran on ourselves**

> **⚠ Provenance & scope (read first).**
> **(1) This addendum covers wave-24's *doctrine and structure*, not its experiments.** At the time of writing, w24's seven spokes are **in flight, 0/7 reviewed** (`handover §10`, 2026-07-24 later). Sources are the **Head+Advisor joint direction doc** (`outputs/head-advisor-w23-direction.md`, Parts 1–4 — Head positions are binding program direction), the **Hub's §10 w24 entry** (the reframe, the Head rulings, the wave table), and the six **w23 output reports** for every number. ⇒ **A second w24 addendum (part 2) is owed when the wave's outputs are reviewed** — `write-ceiling-break`, `headroom-retry-benchmark`, `multi-seed-w23`, `lattice-capacity-theory`, `unlearning-recon` and `phi-stream-discipline` all land in Chapter 8's dials and will move its numbers. **Do not read this addendum as the wave's result.**
> **(2) ⟲ protocol respected:** Chapters 1–7 are **not rewritten** — each carries a new dated `⟲ w24 pointer` blockquote and nothing was removed. **One structural exception, Head-ruled and Hub-specified: Chapter 8 was opened** (the only chapter not authored at w3), plus a dated row-8 addition beneath the original Scorecard and a dated row addition beneath the threads→papers table. **Both additions are appended below the originals, which are untouched.**
> **(3) Companion docs this pass: none** — file ownership was split for parallel safety (`HEP_primers.md` / `research_roadmap.md` / `future_work.md` belong to `doc-curator-w24-plainlang` this wave; this pass owns `philosophy-synthesis.md` **exclusively**, including this addendum).

## ⭐⭐ THE REFRAME, RECORDED AS A REFRAME — with its cause

**The program changed its thesis this wave. That is rare enough that the ledger records the change, the cause, and the boundary of the change separately.**

**The cause is a single measurement: the φ laundering control (N89).** For three waves the standing defence of every loss was *"the store only loses because there is no learned embedding — we are running attention on raw pixels."* Wave-23 built the embedding, twice, in two independently-fair arms, and ran the **pre-registered laundering control**: same features, dumbest possible store. **kNN-in-φ ties or beats CLU-in-φ on all four (dataset × arm) cells, max CLU margin 0.000.** *The win is φ's, not ours.* The escape hatch closed, and with it the thesis it was protecting.

**What changed — the CLAIM rule (new, w24).** The organising thesis moves from *"a designed addressable store that wins retrieval"* to ⭐ **"control over memory in time"**: the store's value is **not static lookup** — that is ceiling-capped by the very operation settling approximates — but **admission · lifetimes · isolation under sequential writes · compute-adaptive reads.** **Every future claim must be a claim about one of those four dials, each backed by a law.**

**What survived — the ARCHITECTURE rule (w23, intact).** The phase doctrine *"learn around a designed core"* (fixed physics + designed store, learned `φ`/`ψ` interfaces + controller policy; attention's fixed `softmax(QKᵀ)V` with learned projections as the precedent) is **not superseded**. It survives, demoted from *thesis* to *architecture rule* — and it is **validated by the same report that killed the lookup claim**: `φ` lifts CLU from CIFAR **chance (0.012) to 0.81–0.97**, and CLU-in-φ beats closed-form Hopfield-in-φ decisively on the **noise** axis in both datasets (MNIST σ=0.4: **0.852 vs 0.250**). **The premise held; the consequence did not.**

**Stated as the supersession the ⟲ protocol requires:** *"learn around a designed core" as the program's **organising thesis** is SUPERSEDED by "control over memory in time" (w24). It is NOT retired — it is re-scoped to the architecture layer. A doc that quotes it as the answer to "what is the program's claim?" is stale; a doc that quotes it as the answer to "how is the system built?" is current.*

**And the one-slide pitch that now travels with the thesis:** ***write = dig a valley · read = drop a ball · forget = let it fill in*** — with the standing w22 precision attached: **a frozen surface does not decay on its own**; "fill in" is a within-rollout fade or an **active controller edit**.

## ⭐ HEAD RULINGS RECORDED (binding; source `head-advisor-w23-direction.md` Part 2 + `handover §10` 2026-07-24)

1. **[RULING] Masked recall → PERMANENTLY APPENDIX-ONLY.** Its role is *"CLU sits near the NN ceiling it approximates, and decisively beats modern Hopfield at load/noise"* — **competence, not victory.** It is the wrong lead task because CLU **approximates** the method that wins it. **No lead claim on that axis again.**
2. **[RULING] The binding scope caveat, verbatim in force:** *masked/static retrieval is a task where **equalling a simple baseline is our best case**, because CLU approximates the nearest-neighbour method that wins it.* **Do not re-litigate.**
3. **[RULING] Boundary — the demotion does NOT apply to the capacity law (R2).** That is *a law about the primitive*, not a competitive claim, and is **exempt**. ⚠ **But its figure must never be framed as beating anything.**
4. 🔴 **Two corrected wordings, both errors caught at review — recorded so the direction doc is never quoted un-corrected.** (i) The NN-ceiling gap is **3.5–17.6 pp on mask, widening to 42 pp under noise** — the direction doc's *"3–13 pp"* matches only **3 of 8 cells** (N90 verbatim: mask −5.5 / −13.3 / −3.5 / −17.6; noise −7.8 / −26.6 / −11.3 / −42.2). (ii) The Hopfield-in-φ margin is **CIFAR-scoped on the capacity axis** — on **MNIST at high load Hopfield is slightly ahead (0.957 vs 0.871)** — though the **noise** margin holds on both datasets. ⭐ *The second error was the Hub's own w23 summary, and the curator caught it against `phi-read-in`'s own prereg scorecard (marked ◐).*
5. **[RULING] φ-stream discipline:** task-1-only `φ` = **PRIMARY**; generic-frozen = a **declared upper bound, never the headline**; online `φ` = later, as its own experiment.
6. **[RULING] CL filing rule:** winning the replay-free class *while sitting below replay* **IS a publishable success**; the replay-handicapped regimes (strict online · tiny budgets · 20–50 tasks · **privacy-constrained, where replay is illegal by rule**) are a **strengthening follow-up, NOT a rescue.**
7. **Multi-seed before any paper number**, and **retry's headroom benchmark must be built from AMBIGUITY, not destruction** (the boost cannot recover past the basin cliff).
8. **Standing:** the **laundering control is mandatory on every future performance claim**; the **under-trained-diagnostics rule** (N94) is in force.

## Curator-flagged doc-hygiene, and what I think the Hub's structural spec got wrong

*(The w23 pass's most valuable output was catching a Hub error; this section is written in that spirit. **Nothing below is adjudicated** — flags for the Hub, not resolutions.)*

1. **⚠ "best-of-five" is a comparison across five METHODS, not five seeds — and the run is 5-seed, which makes the ambiguity dangerous.** The source (`controller-mvp` §2) reads *"per-ADMITTED = 1.000 flat at every K, best of five"*, i.e. best among the compared systems (controller + gru / mlp / attention / learned-CLU). The task spec's *"per-admitted 1.000 flat to K=64, best-of-five"* is inherited verbatim into the Hub's §10 entry. **Ch. 8 §5.2 states the disambiguation inline.** A draft reading it as "best of five seeds" would be quoting a different, weaker fact.
2. **⚠ The admission law as stated is 2-D-specific, and the spec does not say so.** `N_pack = πR²/((√3/2)·d_safe²)` is a **hexagonal-packing count on a disk** (a 2-D formula), and `R = 0.808·√K` is exactly its inverse (`d_safe·√(K·(√3/2)/π) = 0.809·√K` at `d_safe = 1.54` ⇒ `N_pack ≈ K`; verified arithmetically this pass). The store it was measured on is **dim = 3** with disk proposals. **There is no measured d-dimensional admission bound**, and the achieved packing falls short of the ideal in both arms (**5.2 / 6.12** fixed; **42.8 / 64** sized — the `d_eff < d` shell-concentration shortfall, N93). ⇒ **"the packing/admission bound" should not be presented as a general law of the store until the d-dimensional version is measured.** Recorded as a scope line in Ch. 8 §2.
3. **⚠ "capacity doubles per dimension (`2^d`) — exactly the designed rate" is true of the RATE and false of the VALUE.** Designed is `4·2^d`; learned is `2^d` — a constant **4× prefactor gap** for d≤5 (the tax then plateaus ~1/8). The phrasing survives a careful reader and misleads a fast one. **Ch. 8 §2 carries the precision note.**
4. **⚠ The verdict `control validated` is tagged without scope, against this ledger's own two-layer convention.** Chapters 1 and 3 carry `[validated-with-scope]` for results with *more* replication. All four dials here are designed-store, one geometry family; the compute dial is **seed 0**. **I recorded the Head's verdict verbatim and added a scope note beneath it (Ch. 8 §6) rather than altering a ruled verdict** — but if the Hub wants the tag scheme applied consistently, the row should read `[split — lookup refuted, indexing refuted, control validated-with-scope]`. **Hub call.**
5. **⚠ The named ICLR main text lists two components that do not exist.** *"the primitive + three laws + the CL sweep + the unlearning demo"* — **the three laws exist; the CL sweep (R4) and the unlearning demo (R1) are targets** on the Phase-2/3 pathway. Ch. 8 §7 names the vehicle and flags the cargo. A chapter listing the paper's contents without that flag would be the ledger's first promissory note in lead position (**Positioning Charter C-4**).
6. **⚠ One place where the direction doc and the source reports differ in EMPHASIS, not in fact (flagged, not resolved — carried from the w23 pass, still open).** The reports state the retry and controller results as *capability/mechanism* claims **with the losing headline stated first**; the direction doc lists them under positives. **Same facts, different order.** The registry (N90, N91) keeps the loss adjacent to the win in every entry, and Chapter 8 leads with the negatives per the Head's own framing. **The ordering is a rhetorical choice for the Hub; the ledger has made its choice explicit rather than silently.**
7. **⚠ Ch. 8's dial-2 evidence is a hand-driven schedule, not autonomous physics — and R1 ("certified per-item lifetimes") is the claim most exposed by it.** The decay is exact (`exp(−0.35t)`, half-life 1.98) but it is **the controller lowering well depth on a schedule**; the w22 precision (*a frozen surface does not decay on its own*) is still binding. **Recorded in Ch. 8 §1 and §5.2.** If `unlearning-recon` reports that "certified" deletion has a formal bar, **this is the sentence a referee will attack first.**
8. **No output↔handover numeric disagreement found this pass** — with the caveat that this pass checked the **w23 reports** against the **w24 handover entry and the direction doc**, since w24's own outputs do not exist yet. **The two corrected wordings in Head-ruling 4 are the Hub's own corrections, already in §10; transcribed here, not re-adjudicated.**

## ⭐⭐ THE ONE THING THAT MUST NOT BE SETTLED — the write-ceiling mechanism is CONTESTED

**Two w24 reports give competing, live accounts of *what the K≈32 write ceiling IS*, and the ledger records both as live rather than choosing.** This is the wave's single most important registry-discipline call (Hub-ruled).

- **`write-ceiling-break`** measured that **no write operator raises the ceiling** (masked/sequential writes *backfire*, collapsing capacity below 32; scale-invariant and crowding-aware also fail), and reads the survivor as *"optimization interference / a representational limit of the static write"* — a **write-side addressing** failure (basin ≈ strict at every failing cell). It claims its scale-invariance ablation (`|Δ|=0.010`) *"closes the numerics hypothesis."*
- **`lattice-capacity-theory` §4.2** offers a competing **geometric width-lock** account: the critical separation is `sep_crit ≈ 2.4–3.0 × well width` at **every** designed AND learned wall d = 2…8, which — with `minsep ∝ K^{-1/d}` flattening in high d — **predicts a d-independent-looking ceiling *without* an operator ceiling**, and reproduces the learned/designed tax as `2^{-d}` from the shipped `well_width 0.15` vs `atom_init_width 0.30` (w23 measured 1/4, 1/8, 1/16 at d = 2, 3, 4).
- **The scale-invariance arm rescaled DEPTHS/MARGINS/BARRIER, not WIDTH+RADIUS**, so it does **not** test §4.2.

**⇒ RULING (Hub, applied):** N92's phrase *"the ceiling belongs to the WRITE OPERATOR"* now carries the qualifier **"(mechanism CONTESTED: a geometric width-lock account is under test; decisive check is the free §5.0 trained-`log_width` dump)."** **What IS settled by measurement:** scale-dilution is ruled out (`|Δ|=0.010`) — so the *"not numerics"* clause of the Head's atoms record (primers §11.14) can be marked *"scale-dilution ruled out by measurement."* **What is NOT settled:** whether the ceiling is the write operator or a fixed-width geometric lock. The `lattice §5.0` (trained-`log_width` dump) and `§5.2` (`atom_init_width 0.30→0.15`, predicted ×`2^d`) experiments are the adjudicator. Registered as **N92 (w24 update) / N96**; do not re-word either way.

A draft reading it as "best of five seeds" is quoting a weaker fact.

## Dial-by-dial deltas — where each w24 output lands in Chapter 8

**§2 Theory / §1 — ⭐ NEW ENTRY: Proposition L2 (sharding), proven + measured (`lattice-capacity-theory`).**
> **Prop L2.** AdamW's `(m,v)` state is per-parameter, so under a masked write on disjoint atom groups the optimizer factorizes exactly into blocks: **N independent optimizers ≡ one masked joint optimizer** (measured bit-identical to the site tail; `checkB`). ⇒ **the Head's "chain optimizers / map disconnected optimizer spaces" worry has no referent — a masked write on a block-diagonal problem IS N independent optimizers.** BUT the capacity law is **`K_total = min(K_addr(geometry, s, σ_q), N·K_ceiling)`** — sharding **relocates the binding constraint from the write operator onto geometry and can NEVER exceed geometry** — and it **requires a global address allocator** (a registry, not an optimizer). Two companion propositions: **Prop L4** (sharding conserves read-side discrimination — for the shipped mixture store `n_groups` the read is *literally identical* to monolithic ⇒ read cost is **O(1) in N exactly**; a product store is O(1) in rollout depth, O(N) in pointwise scores, ~N/2400 of one read ⇒ free to N≈100); and the **routing verdict** — ⛔ **never route on post-settle energy** (at or below chance at equal well depth, N97); route on pre-settle energy (R2) or settling displacement (R3), both exact to N=8 with interleaved shards. *Status: [proven for the map; the only open question is W3 (loss-gradient separability), which fails in practice ONLY through the atom-init defect, N98 — fixable].* ⚠ **The routing half is classical nearest-neighbour indexing — "capacity multiplies by sharding" must never be presented as a dynamical result (N89 discipline).** Registry: N97 (post-settle energy), N98 (the init defect + its fix).

**§5.1(2) φ successor / §2 — the φ-stream discipline result (`phi-stream-discipline`).** The w23 φ subsection's successor experiment landed: on a Split-MNIST stream (5×2, M=160, 3 seeds), a **leakage-free `task1_only` φ costs ≈0 identity accuracy end-of-stream (−0.023 PCA / +0.006 AE at `phi_dim=32`)** ⇒ **`task1_only` φ is RATIFIED as primary; w25's CL entry does NOT need online φ.** The cost is a *tilt* across task index (+0.034…+0.077/task), not a growing deficit — a narrow φ **out-resolves** a broad φ on its own dense classes (gap −0.115 at task 0). ⚠ **NEW binding protocol parameter: `phi_dim ≥ 16` for any strict-φ claim** — the null is *capacity slack*, not absence of cost (gap +0.117/+0.190 at `phi_dim=4`; the strict basis captures 10–18 pp less later-task variance at every dim). **N89 UPHELD + EXTENDED:** laundering fires in all 4 headline + all 16 stress cells; the store-advantage-under-strictness watch-item does NOT fire (delta −0.004…+0.019). ⚠ Everything MNIST; Split-CIFAR-10 (where a strict φ should bite) is the named open rung.

**§5.1(3) / §5.3 — the write ceiling survives every lever (`write-ceiling-break`, N96).** §5.1(3)'s "~32 write ceiling" refutation gains its gate result: **the ceiling SURVIVES masked/sequential (which backfire), scale-invariant, and crowding-aware writes at d∈{4,6,8}** — no lever raised `K_ceiling`, and the sequential/masked family *lowered* it. This **closes N92's "single highest-value untested lever"** with a NO, and shows the law `min(2^d, ~32)` is **robust to the write operator** — ⚠ *but see the CONTESTED-mechanism ruling above; §5.3's geometry-vindication for d≤5 is unaffected.*

**§5.2 dial 4 / dial 2 — the headroom retry NO, with headroom present (`headroom-retry-benchmark`, N95).** Dial 4 (compute-adaptive reads) gains the decisive R3 test: **built from ambiguity** (block occlusion + contracted crowded store, 3 seeds, first-pass 0.26–0.93 off ceiling), **CLU-gated retry still does NOT beat the ML-optimal `feedforward_nn_masked` oracle (=1.000) in any of 8 cells (−3.5…−38 pp)** — so CM-23(b)'s absolute-dominance retraction **cannot be blamed on saturation.** The mechanism half survives with teeth (kick/ensemble flat-or-declining; boost +2.3…+36.2 pp; auto-stops ×1.20–1.81). ⭐ **General lesson for dial 4:** on a designed store, identity retrieval under *known* erasure is information-theoretically trivial (the masked oracle is 1.000 regardless of ambiguity), so "ambiguity" only bites methods that ignore the mask — **the one untested route to a win is a query in a space the store is NOT metric-native to (cross-modal / φ-latent).** RUD-C mandatory baseline #1 = the masked observed-dims oracle.

**§5.2 compute row / §6 scope note — the multi-seed hardening (`multi-seed-w23`).** §5.2's compute-dial row and §6's scope note both firm up: **N89 and N90 SURVIVE at 5 seeds (0 violations of pre-registered bars)** — laundering fires 4/4 cells at every seed (0 CLU wins in 22 pairs); mechanism controls 0/40 violations each. The compute row's numbers restate: **gated lift +0…+79.7 pp, saturation mean ×1.53 (range ×1.0–1.8).** **N92's low-`d` walls get a third seed: d=4=16 SURVIVES (0.928); d=5=32 SOFTENS (0.898 mean, straddling 0.90)** ⇒ quote the low-`d` regime as **"≥16, consistent with 32", NOT a clean 32.** ⚠ **The §6 scope note's seed exposure improves from "seed-0/2-seed" to "seed-0 (dial 4) / 3–5-seed (dials 1–3, the laundering and mechanism results)".**

**§7 R1 reframe (`unlearning-recon`, N99).** The paper-vehicle section's R1 target is reframed at the quote-block level: ⛔ **DELETE "certified"** — it formally means (ε,δ)-indistinguishability from retraining (Guo et al. ICML 2020, Def 1–2), and CLU satisfies none of the four requirements; **"deletion by construction" is OCCUPIED** (SILO ICLR'24, SISA, Ticketed L-U, PALL ICLR'25); and ⛔ **a NEW blocking technical gap** — the MVC-0 controller's refuse-and-relocate + LRU eviction make the store history-dependent, so CLU cannot claim even **exact** deletion without an order-independent placement rule (N99). **Approved reframe: file R1 as a *memory-control* result — "scheduled per-item retention / set-at-write-time lifetimes with an exact decay law" — with unlearning as motivation and an explicit "no (ε,δ) claim" sentence.** ⚠ **PALL (ICLR 2025)** joins SQHN on the `continual-learning-recon` must-cite/differentiate list (it occupies the CL∩unlearning cell w25 enters). Naming hazard: never "unlearning" for CLU's mechanism (Hopfield/Feinstein/Palmer 1983 = anti-Hebbian dream-phase removal; CLU has a sleep phase) — use deletion/erasure/scheduled forgetting. Forbidden-claim consequences → CM-22(m)/(n)/(o).

## ⛔ The packing-slack retraction (applied)

**"w23 iid store ran at packing slack ≈1.08" is a UNIT ARTIFACT** (`headroom-retry-benchmark` §A1, `lattice`): the metric compared a per-element σ_q against a vector median-NN (a √D≈28 unit mismatch), pinning the slack at the tautology `1/(3.1·clu_s_frac)` for *every* store. **Corrected slack for w23 iid mask p=0.5 M=128 = 0.227 ⇒ w23 already ran PAST the packing bound** (not at it). This hits `phi-read-in.md` §5 (which quoted slack ≡1.08) and the N89 mechanism line ("`spacing/Δ_req ≡ 1.08` by construction"). ⛔ **Never quote 1.08 as a measured store property** — the §7.19 "never quote 2.6" precedent. N89's *conclusion* is unaffected (the store still launders; running *past* the bound is a stronger statement of the same crowding).

# ⟲ Wave-25 addendum — the CONTACT wave (2026-07-28, doc-curator `w25-sync`) — **the four dials met a real benchmark and a real adversary in the same wave; the store's absolute numbers were owned by trivial baselines in both, and what survived contact was the mechanism set — including a mechanism nobody had written down**

> **⚠ Provenance & scope (read first).**
> **(1) Sources.** The five w25 output reports (`r2-geometry-revival`, `cl-entry-build`, `lattice-sharded-store`, `order-independent-placement`, `mia-decay-measurement`), the Hub's §10 **wave-25 review entry** (2026-07-26; **reviewed 5/5, all ACCEPTED, integrated → main GREEN 701**) and its **2026-07-28** entry (Addendum 2 applied; wave-26 scoped). **Every number below is transcribed from a report with its section; no verdict is reinterpreted.** Where a report and the handover differ in *emphasis* it is flagged, not resolved (see doc-hygiene).
> **(2) ⟲ protocol respected.** Chapters 1–8 are **not rewritten.** Chapter 8's §-bodies keep their w19–w24 text; this addendum moves its dials by addendum, exactly as parts 1 and 2 did. **Nothing deleted anywhere.** Two dated pointers are added below (Ch. 5, Ch. 7) and nothing else in the chapter bodies is touched.
> **(3) The Hub did NOT write a w25 addendum before this pass** (the last entry in this file was the w24 part-2 addendum) ⇒ this is the wave's primary ledger record, not a supplement.
> **(4) Companion docs this pass:** `negative_results.md` (N100–N108 + six dated amendment blocks), `claims_matrix.md` (→ **v2.4**), `research_roadmap.md` (→ **v0.8**), `HEP_primers.md` (§11.6/§11.14 updates + **§11.15**), `future_work.md` (w25 fold). This pass owns all six.

## ⭐⭐ THE WAVE IN ONE ARC

**Four things happened, and they rhyme.**

**(1) A two-year-old question was settled by a third answer.** For two waves the program carried an explicitly CONTESTED mechanism: is the K≈32 learned-capacity ceiling *the write operator* (`write-ceiling-break`) or *a geometric width lock* (`lattice §4.2`)? The w24 review refused to settle it and named the adjudicator: a **free** dump of the trained `log_width`. The dump ran — and **the geometric account passed its registered test handsomely** (a *perfect* d ≥ 4 PASS/FAIL classifier, boundary **2.30 | 2.49**, including the out-of-sample d=8 K=64 crack cell; widths 0.215–0.364, never near the 0.18 falsifier). That pass **authorised** the causal experiment, which then **refuted the account that authorised it**: `atom_init_width 0.30 → 0.15` moves the wall **downward**, `K_learned(4)` below 16 (**0.9368 ± 0.0133 → 0.5944 ± 0.0164**, 3 seeds, **28 SE**, flat under a 2× atom re-check). The probes that followed found the real constraint, and it is **neither contestant**: the read launches on the **payload = 0 manifold**, so a narrow well is *deep, sharp and invisible from the launch point* — **a well-width ↔ read-out-excursion REACH condition**, `s ≳ σ_q` below and **`s ≳ |a|_max/κ` (κ ≈ 3)** above. Confirmed by intervention, not inferred: halving the excursion (tolerance halved with it) takes **d=4 K=32 — w23's *firm* wall, flat across a 16× atom sweep — from 0.824 to 1.000**, and d=4 K=64 to **0.9922**, *at both widths*. **The Head and two advisors called this the most important scientific result the program has produced.**

**(2) The CL entry exists — and the four-dial thesis survived contact with a real benchmark *exactly as the pattern predicted*.** On Split-MNIST Class-IL the entry is **the best rehearsal-free method (+0.510 over EWC)** — *in a class whose incumbents fail by construction* — sits **−0.153 below iCaRL**, and is **laundered by a kNN-in-φ ring buffer at matched memory (−0.036)**: the **fourth consecutive wave** in which a trivial baseline owns the headline number. And, again exactly as the pattern predicts, **CLU owns the axis the baselines structurally lack**: forgetting. **BWT −0.169 vs −0.99** for the parametric class, and **better than tuned ER at the same item budget (0.169 vs 0.264) with 24.5× fewer floats.** ⛔ Split-CIFAR-10 is a **NULL** (0.149, below LwF 0.162) — and the diagnosis is clean and unflattering in a *useful* direction: **the feature space, not the stream discipline** (kNN over the same φ reaches 0.21; strict-φ cost **+0.001** — strictness does **not** bite, refuting w24's own expectation; the store's retrieval on those addresses is 1.000). *The memory works; what it holds is uninformative about the label.*

**(3) Deletion went from "blocked by history-dependence" to "a proven placement rule plus a numeric acceptance test" — in one wave, from both ends.** From the theory end, **PGCP**: `Store(S)` proven a **set function**, `delete = set-minus` **bit-exactly**, decay/permanence **provably commute** with deletion, the spacing certificate free by construction — verified at **zero tolerance** across 24 exhaustive orders, 800 random orders, 100 write/delete interleavings and mid-decay deletes, and byte-equal on the **shipped** store PyTree. **The price of exactness is *negative* on packing** (61/64 vs refuse-and-relocate's 42.8/64) and positive on churn (2.84 moves/delete at full load). From the measurement end, the **magnitude of the gap it must close**: under the shipped placement the **allocator trace is a near-perfect post-evict membership oracle — `AUC(z_hole) = 0.99985`, TPR 1.000 @ FPR 1 %** — *after* the item is gone from `V`, while with placement held fixed every post-evict statistic is **0.5000 ± 0.0000**. ⇒ **the amplitude channel closes exactly and the allocator channel does not**, and R1 now has something it never had: **a number to hit.**

**(4) The lifetimes dial got its adversarial accounting, and it is honest in both directions.** ⛔ Against an **exact** adversary, amplitude decay buys **nothing at any point in an item's life** (white-box AUC **1.000 at all 18 levels**; what decays is the *effect size*, `gap = 0.3935·A` to 4 dp, `d′` 1560 → 79.6) — **the dial declaration's falsifier fired, exactly as pre-registered.** ✅ Against a **resolution-limited** adversary the grading is real and the pre-registered law survives to ~2 % (`A₇₅ = 2.57·σ_obs`). ⭐ And the differentiator that needs **no adversary caveat at all** is **retrieval geometry**: decay *contracts the addressing tolerance* of an item (`R₅₀` **1.146 → 0.752**, 1.52×) where a timestamped row is a constant step at ≈0.77. ⭐ The sentence the wave earned: ***the store stops answering before it stops leaking*** (retention **0.832** vs query-AUC **0.983** at the last amplitude before self-eviction; the curves never cross).

**The rhyme.** In all four, *the honest instrument was built before the claim* — a free width dump, a laundering control run in two forms, a permutation harness at zero tolerance, a paired-placement OUT world that had to return exactly 0.5 and did. **Three of the four produced a result its own author had pre-registered against.**

## ⭐ Dial-by-dial deltas — where each w25 output lands in Chapter 8

**§2 theory / §5.3 capacity — ⭐⭐ the ceiling's attribution is SETTLED, and it is a new object (`r2-geometry-revival`, N100).** Chapter 8's capacity material carries the w24 CONTESTED flag; **that flag is now cleared with a third answer.** The design rule to record — *stated with no comparison to anything, per the R2 exemption* — is: **the well width is set by the read-out channel's excursion, not by the address-space packing.** `s` must satisfy **both** `s ≳ σ_q` (the basin must contain the jittered query — the floor the theorist identified) **and** `s ≳ |a|_max/κ` with κ = O(3) (the basin must be visible from the launch manifold — *the ceiling-side condition nobody had written down*). With `|a|_max = 1` the second binds at `s ≈ 0.30`: exactly the shipped default, and exactly where the write pushes any narrower init (+23 % from 0.15). ⇒ **there is no room underneath it, so there is no width route to R2.** It also explains why both prior accounts were *partly right*: `sep/width` correlates with the wall because numerator and denominator both drift smoothly with d, and the ceiling *looks* d-independent because `|a|_max = 1` is d-independent. ⚠ **Fairness caveat that travels with every sentence about the excursion cells: they are a MECHANISM result at a different task point, not a capacity number** — this harness's payload channel has **no read-out noise**, so shrinking the excursion is free *here*. ⛔ **No `K_learned` at `pscale ≠ 1`.**

**§5.3 capacity (second entry) — sharding is real on the write side and partly spent on the read side (`lattice-sharded-store`, N105–N107).** Prop L2 stops being theory: shards are **separate parameter trees**, so "N independent optimizers" is not approximated, it is **literal**, and **d=6 K=64 is FIXED by sharding** (**0.9251 ± 0.0030** vs the monolithic laundering line at identical init and identical total atoms, **0.8802 ± 0.0075**; **0.9043 vs 0.8288** registry-routed with no init change). But the geometry-bound **d=4 K=32 control is not fixed even with routing removed** (0.8932 / 0.8984 — *quote as "0.89–0.90, does not clear 0.90"*) ⇒ **`K_total = min(K_addr, Σ_r K_write)`; `K = 32N` is NOT unclamped.** ⭐ The interpretable half: **at d=6, ~60 % of the monolithic store's deficit from the bar is ROUTING, not writing.** Two costs are now on the record — the atom floor is **per-STORE** (matched sharding is automatically N× starved ⇒ its wins are *conservative* and its N ≥ 4 collapses are **not** capacity results) — and one free gift: **the sharded write is 4–5× cheaper wall-clock** (`O(K²)` barrier → `O(K²/N)`). ⛔ **Never "capacity multiplies by sharding" as a dynamical claim**: the routing half is classical indexing, and **a classical kNN over the registry solves the routing task outright** (stated up front by the source, not buried).

**§5.2 dial 4 — ⭐ the first non-losing anytime read, and it is a TIE (`cl-entry-build` Item 3, N103).** The w24 lesson said the one untested route was *a query in a space the store is not metric-native to*. The CL entry supplies it for free: corruption in **pixel space**, store addressed in **φ**, so the `feedforward_nn_masked` oracle that beat CLU in 8/8 w24 cells **cannot be constructed**. Result: gated retry **0.885 ± 0.018 @ 1.40 ± 0.20×** vs the matched-compute floor **0.877 ± 0.025** ⇒ **+0.8 ± 1.6 pp, a tie**; mechanism margins large and clean (**+4.6 ± 2.2 pp over random kick in 6/6 seeds**; ensemble +4.4; **ungated collapses to 0.000**). ⭐ **This is the first regime in the program's history where the anytime read is not *below* the correct floor** (w24: −3.5 … −38 pp in 8/8) — and it is a tie **because the author pre-registered that a win here must be re-run, ran seeds 3–5, and reported the tie the re-run produced.** ⚠ Two scope items travel: one dataset/one corruption level, and **τ is not the binding gate element in φ-space** (1 seed; a behavioural difference from w24). ⭐ Free companion that inverts an R3 framing assumption: **there is no forgetting-by-age** — the **newest** items are hardest (0.796), not the oldest (0.846).

**§5.2 dial 2 / §7 R1 — ⭐⭐ the lifetimes dial gets its adversary and its placement rule (`mia-decay-measurement` N108; `order-independent-placement` → N99 update).** Both halves of the laundering verdict must travel: against an exact adversary a physical `exp(−leak·t)` amplitude and a **boolean TTL flag are equally detectable** (1.000 vs 1.000 white-box; 0.983 vs 1.000 query) — *the "physical, not bookkeeping" differentiator does not exist at the AUC level* — while at `σ_obs = 0.1` they separate by **0.437**, and in **retrieval geometry** they separate with no adversary at all (`R₅₀` 1.146 → 0.752 vs a constant 0.77). ⚠ **Chapter 8 §5.2's dial-2 "hand-driven schedule" caveat (w24 critique item 7) is now sharpened by measurement, not softened:** the decay is exact, and it is *also* **payload-dependent by construction** (`r = −0.846` with `a_i²`: at the floor, retention is 0.999 for `a = ±0.1429` but **0.500 for `a = +1`**), because the `q₂(0) = 0` guard makes each site a payload **hill** of height `0.5κa_i²` against a well of depth `A`. **Two items given the same `leak` do not have the same effective lifetime** — a defect of the *dial*, not of the decay law (the law is exact to 6.8e-8 on the live CL stream, and permanent items ride through all five tasks at retention 1.000). ⇒ **R1's cleanest current sentence is not about privacy at all**; it is *"the store stops answering before it stops leaking"* plus *"decay contracts an item's addressing tolerance."*

**§8 epistemics — three new exemplars, all about instruments.** (i) ⭐ **"The free check that authorised the experiment that killed it."** Stage 0 **passed** its falsifiers and *therefore* licensed Stage 1, which refuted the causal claim in the opposite direction — with **three pre-registrations failing as registered in one task** (the theorist's S1.1–S1.3 *and* the engineer's own S1.4 central estimate and S1.5 magnitude). *A staged design in which the early stage can only authorise, never confirm.* (ii) ⭐ **A falsifier that fired as pre-registered is the cleanest way to own a negative** — `mia-decay-measurement` registered *"the falsifier will fire on AUC"* **and it did, to 4 dp**, so the wave's most uncomfortable number arrives as a **confirmed prediction, not a surprise**. (iii) ⚠ **Two controls in this wave were found to be broken *as controls* before they were used** — the w24 `_feedforward_ladder` clip-TTA is **destructive on signed φ vectors** (it was never a floor; the corrected `_feedforward_ladder_phi` replaces it and the invalid line is retained under an explicit `INVALID_in_phi` name), and `item_decay_demo.retention_of` scores the basin against a **one-element site array**, making that criterion vacuous. *Both were caught by someone re-deriving a number rather than inheriting it — the same pattern as w13's N51 and w23's N94, now at the level of the **control** rather than the measurement.*

## Curator-flagged doc-hygiene (not contradictions — Hub matters)

1. **⚠ One report↔report tension, recorded as an EXTENSION rather than a conflict — flagged so nobody reads it as a silent resolution.** `lattice-sharded-store` measures **R3 (settling displacement) broken** on the shipped `BallRegisterPotential` (1.000/0.549/0.236/0.228) while **N97 records R3 exact (1.000)** on analytic Gaussian stores under gradient-flow settling. **Both are correct under their own scope, and N97's own scope line anticipated it** (*"R3's constants could shift under the true settling path"*). The registry records N104 as an **extension**; **neither set of numbers is retired.** *If the Hub prefers this filed as a contradiction, that is a Hub call — the curator did not adjudicate it.*
2. **⚠ A report↔ruling separation the drafts must preserve.** `cl-entry-build` explicitly **declined to soften** its own headline (*"whether this counts as an external benchmark won is a Hub/Head call, not mine; I am handing over the measurement"*), and the Head then ruled **NO for the score sentence**. The registry and matrix record **the measurement and the ruling separately** rather than merging them. **A draft must carry both** — the measurement is real, and the filing decision is what makes it supplementary.
3. **⚠ The wave's most likely misquotation, stated in advance.** *"Halving the read-out excursion takes the w23 wall from 0.824 to 1.000"* is a **mechanism sentence at a different task point** and will be read as a capacity result by anyone skimming. It has three guards (no `K_learned` at pscale ≠ 1 · the noiseless-payload caveat · the fairness conditions on the successor experiment) and **all three must travel with it in every doc.** *This is the "2^d exactly the designed rate" problem from w24 part 1, one wave later and with higher stakes.*
4. **⚠ Two shipped-code defects that move WORDING but no measured number, and one that moves a row.** D1 (`evict` leaves `centers`/`payloads` verbatim) → *"the item is removed from `V`; the row is not cleared"* — **no physics number moves.** D3 (payload-dependent lifetimes) → **conceptual; needs a theorist ruling before the potential is touched.** **D2 moves a row**: `controller-mvp` §3(b)/N91's decay-demo retention line (corrected above).
5. **⚠ The seed exposure is the wave's weakest flank, and it is concentrated in the *most* important result.** N100's decisive Stage-1 cell is 3 seeds at 28 SE — but **Stage 0 is 1 seed per cell** (the perfect classifier and the 2.30–2.49 boundary carry **no error bars**) and **the excursion cells are 1 seed, written-only, with no blank control** (declared). The verdict margins are large; the *digits* are thin. **A 3-seed repeat of d=6 K=64 and d=8 K=64 would tighten the one table most likely to become a figure.**
6. **⚠ Load-bearing citations asserted from training knowledge.** PGCP's novelty sentence rests on Naor–Teague / Hartline / Blelloch–Golovin / Micciancio / Karger, **none verified from primaries** (the theorist flagged this himself). `deletion-prior-art` (w26) gates the wording — and the *expected* finding (**the discrete skeleton IS prior art**) is stated in advance so it cannot later be reported as a discovery or as a disappointment.
7. **⚠ A structural observation for the Hub, offered not resolved.** Chapter 8 now carries **three addenda and no revision of its §-bodies** — which is the ⟲ protocol working as designed, but it means a reader must assemble the current state of each dial from four places. **The chapter is not yet wrong; it is becoming expensive to read.** If w26's contested-win experiment lands either way, that is the natural moment for the Hub to author a **Chapter 8 §5 restatement** (append-only, superseding nothing) rather than a fourth addendum. *Recorded as a recommendation with a trigger, in the spirit of the w19–w23 Chapter-8 refusals: the curator does not restructure a document whose structure encodes the program's beliefs.*
8. **No output↔handover numeric disagreement found this pass.** Every headline number in the Hub's §10 w25 entry reproduces digit-for-digit against the five reports; the two places where the handover compresses (the CL "wins" clause and the excursion clause) both carry their qualifiers in the same sentence.

# ⟲ Wave-26 addendum — the wave the reach discovery PAID FOR (2026-07-29, doc-curator `w26-sync`, **[C1W27]**) — **a standing wall moved because the read-out code changed, an exactness claim acquired a code path and a met number, and — on the one clean-query benchmark where the whole system was measured end-to-end — deleting the dynamics made it better**

## ⭐⭐ THE WAVE IN ONE ARC

**Five things happened. Four of them were paid for by the previous wave's discovery, and the fifth was paid for by a control.**

**(1) The wall moved — and it moved because the *read-out code* changed, not the physics.** w25 found that the learned-capacity ceiling is a **reach** condition and closed the width route. w26 asked the only question left — *what if the excursion the read has to travel is itself a design parameter?* — and got two answers at once. The **anisotropic annealed read** (widen **only** the payload channel, on a schedule that returns to the stored landscape) takes the w23 *firm* wall at d=4 K=32 from **0.8210 ± 0.0028 to 0.9997 ± 0.0005** (3 seeds, **~110 SE**) at **zero extra parameters, zero extra latent dimensions, zero extra Verlet steps and no change to the stored format**. The **multi-channel payload code** (Δ held at the 1-channel value, so the item still carries `log₂K` bits at the same per-axis noise) takes `K_learned(4)` to **128 = `K_designed(4)`** ⇒ **the learned/designed prefactor tax that has stood since w23 closes from 1/8 to 1/1 at every noise level measured.** ⭐ **Three controls are what make this a mechanism instead of a knob**, and they are the most instructive part: **`readonly`** (anneal the value phase only) reproduces the baseline **to four decimals** ⇒ *the entire gain is address acquisition* — which finally explains why `basin ≡ strict` at every failing cell since w23; **`static`** (widen and never sharpen) gets **basin 0.9993** and then reads the **wrong value** ⇒ *"wider wells" is a different, failing result and the continuation is load-bearing*; **`spectator`** (the same extra dims and +43 % parameters, code left long) buys **+0.034** where the code buys **+0.195**. And the Head's fairness condition did exactly what it was imposed for: **w25's `pscale` free lunch dies under read noise (0.9995 → 0.5894) where the constant-Δ code passes (0.9980).** ⚠ **d = 4 only. d = 6 and d = 8 were not run; no exponent is re-measured.**

**(2) R1 closed in scope, and it closed the strongest way a claim can.** The acceptance criterion was **a number**, set a wave in advance: drive post-eviction `AUC(z_hole)` from 0.99985 to ~0.5. It went to **0.5000 ± 0.0000 on every statistic**, `TPR@FPR 1 %` to **0.000**, **not because the adversary's statistic got weak but because the two stores are byte-identical in 3 072/3 072 worlds** — so every present *and future* statistic is exactly tied. It survives a live displacement cascade. The rematch under the **real two-phase Verlet read** closed the theorist's own H5 scope gap (per-admitted **1.0000**; per-offered **0.9531**, now quotable **as measured**), so the lattice does not need to inflate and the packing win is not repaid. ⛔ **And the boundary is measured, not asserted:** at overflow the same build is **not** exact (`AUC(n_live)` = 1.000) because a refused background item does not counterfactually return — *that is the un-built waitlist, and it is why the scope sentence is load-bearing.*

**(3) The contested win was taken properly and it failed exactly as its author pre-registered — and then the sharpest negative in the program's history arrived from its own control.** At matched **bytes**, CLU beats the entire replay field on **ACC at 6/6** budgets (**0.903 ± 0.003 vs iCaRL 0.885 ± 0.002**) and on forgetting at **5/6** — *reversing* w25's matched-**items** picture. ⛔ And the **matched-bytes launder is never beaten** (6/6 both axes). ⭐⛔ Then the second launder: **the same keys, the same wells, the same admission and eviction — with the damped-Verlet settle deleted — beats the store 6/6 on forgetting and 6/6 on ACC.** *Nothing else differs.* **On clean-query classification the physics is a measured ≈1 pp net negative.** Every previous laundering could be answered with *"the baseline holds a different memory"*; **this one cannot.**

**(4) Theory superseded its own bound one wave after writing it, and made a design statement out of an embarrassment.** The reach condition is not a threshold on `|a|/s` at all: it is a **saddle-node bifurcation** governed by `L = √(|c_i|² + a_i²)`, verified **±2.5 % on the shipped integrator** and **31/32 items on the trained shipped `V` with zero free parameters** — with the mechanism caught in the act (every failed flow lands on the one item the sampler placed at the ball centre). It yields a quotable no-go — ***reach is logarithmically un-buyable*** (κ 4→5 needs a 55× deeper well; 4→6 needs 9 200×) — and it demotes the program's prettiest correlate: **`sep` alone separates 11/11 Stage-0 cells** where `sep/w` separates only d ≥ 4, so **the ratio is a reparameterisation, not a cause.** And on the lifetimes dial the theorist converted the payload-dependence defect into a **TRILEMMA** (*value fidelity · amplitude-independent hold · amplitude-independent latency — pick two*), showed the shipped store simply **chose a corner**, refuted **both** proposed fixes (one of them with the program's own anti-decoration guard: **the trivial substitute passes 0.672 of queries running no dynamics at all**), refuted **his own first implementation** of the recommended fix — and identified the third corner as **the compute-adaptive-read dial**: *a faded memory costs more integration steps to read.*

**(5) Two carried numbers turned out to be endpoints of curves, and a citation pass corrected our authorship.** The allocator leak is **0.6715 / 0.9165 / 0.9961 / 0.99985** at 2/4/6/8 offers — so *an acceptance test evaluated at low load looks twice as close to done as it is* — with the mechanism pinned (**the IN-world minimum `z_hole` equals `d_safe` to 4 dp at every load**; what moves is how tightly the background packs in the OUT world). The τ contradiction between w24 and w25 **resolved without retracting either**: τ binds **iff `#{cos₀ < τ} < k·step_n`** — bit-identical ladders at p = 0.8, **−0.485 ± 0.064** at p = 0.5 *in the same store* — and the analyst's own pre-registered "it's a φ-space property" hypothesis was **falsified**. Meanwhile the scout found that **Blelloch–Golovin (FOCS'07) own our placement algorithm outright**: our Theorems 1–2 are **instantiations** of their Thm 3.2 + `DELETE`, and **the fix-up cascade is theirs**.

**The rhyme.** w25's rhyme was *the honest instrument was built before the claim.* **w26's is sharper and less comfortable: the controls did the science.** The value-blank control exposed an entire **metric** as vacuous (a blank landscape scoring **1.0000**); the anti-decoration guard **killed a proposed fix before it was built**; the `readonly`/`static` controls **located** the mechanism the headline could not; and the same-keys launder **measured the program's own read as a net negative.** Three of the wave's four most consequential findings came from a control, not from a headline arm.

## ⭐ Dial-by-dial deltas — where each w26 output lands in Chapter 8

**§5.3 capacity / §2 theory — ⭐⭐ the ceiling MOVED, and the criterion that describes it was replaced in the same wave (`r2-excursion-reach` N109–N111; `readout-channel-theory` N120).** Chapter 8's capacity material should now be read as: *the ceiling is a property of the **read-out interface**, a part of the primitive nobody had treated as a design parameter.* Two interventions on that interface move `K_learned(4)` to `K_designed(4)`; the criterion governing them is a **saddle condition on `L`**, not a threshold on `|a|/s`; and the shipped store sits **6 % inside** its own reach boundary (`a_U = 1.06` vs `|a|max = 1.0`), *which is why every one-flag intervention moves it.* Two knobs are re-classified as **reach parameters, not free knobs** (`learned_confine`, the ball radius), and a new measured fact runs **for** the learned arm: the write **adapts per-item well width to payload excursion** (`corr = +0.82`, far-payload wells 1.31× wider), so a constant-width designed surrogate reproduces only 5/11 cells. ⚠ **Everything here is d = 4, and the scope clause is part of the claim.**

**§5.2 dial 1 / §7 R1 — ⭐⭐ the deletion claim became a code path, a met number and a stated boundary (`placement-landing` → N99 w26 block, N112; `deletion-prior-art` → N118).** What changes in the chapter's reading is not the physics but the **claim structure**: with canonical placement, `Controller.delete(i)` has *exactly* the claim structure of `del d[k]` on a canonical dictionary — **and that IS the deletion claim.** The differentiators (the continuous amplitude law with the Thm-4 commutation, the spacing certificate, permanence and decay coexisting in one potential, the packing economics 61 vs 43) are properties **around** the claim and must never be smuggled into it. ⛔ Two boundaries travel: **overflow** (the claim fails; the waitlist is unbuilt) and **attribution** (the algorithm is Blelloch–Golovin's; the defensible novelty is the four-part composition).

**§5.2 dial 2 — ⭐ the lifetimes dial got a ruling, and the ruling is an impossibility result (`readout-channel-theory` → N119).** Chapter 8's dial-2 caveat (*"payload-dependent effective half-life"*, w25) is now **explained rather than apologised for**: it is a corner of a trilemma, forced by the anti-decoration guard plus a fixed read budget. ⭐ The chapter gains a genuinely new statement: **the third corner is dial 4** — *"a faded memory costs more integration steps to read"* — which is the first time two of the four dials have been shown to be **the same trade-off seen from different sides**.

**§5.2 dial 4 — ◐ scoped, not moved (`carried-remeasurements` → N117).** The tie stands (gated 0.8631 vs the matched-compute floor 0.8698 at p = 0.8) and is **not upgraded**. What changes is that the gate's *threshold* clause acquires a **rule** in place of a claim about a space, and the rule is exact arithmetic: `#{cos₀ < τ}` against `k·step_n`. ⚠ **The quantity that decides the question is not saved by the harness**, and the τ-sweep runs only on the last corruption level — *which is exactly why the contradiction survived a wave.*

**§5.1 / §6 the benchmark face — ⭐⛔ the honest position sharpened by one word: "settle" (`matched-bytes-frontier` → N113–N116; `cl-encoder` → N121–N122).** The w23 shape (*"the baseline owns the headline, CLU owns a mechanism axis"*) held again — but the w26 form is more specific and more useful: **the baseline is now the store itself with its dynamics removed.** The chapter's honest sentence becomes *"the settle costs you when the query is clean and pays when it is ambiguous"*, which is smaller than "the memory with dials" **and better evidenced than anything else the program has**. Alongside it, two results that are not about winning at all: the store **saturating gate-bound at 6 085/7 658 wells** (the packing law appearing inside a benchmark) and the φ gate **straddling** at 2.5× fit compute, with **the strict-φ cost finally biting (+0.020 ± 0.010) exactly across the decision boundary**.

**§8 epistemics — three new exemplars, all about controls.** (i) ⭐ **A control that kills a proposed fix.** The anti-decoration guard was written to stop the *store* from cheating; in w26 it stopped **us** from shipping a fix that would have deleted the lifetimes dial's value-side signal, before a line of it was written. (ii) ⭐ **A control that invalidates a metric.** The value-blank control showed the shipped `tol` criterion scoring a **blank landscape at 1.0000** at m > 1 — *the criterion, not the result, was the thing that was wrong* — and it is precisely the hole w25's `pscale` probe fell through. (iii) ⭐⛔ **A control that measures the thesis.** The same-keys launder is the first control in the program's history whose *only* difference from the system is **the physics**, and it says the physics costs ≈1 pp on this benchmark. *A control at that level of specificity is not a check on a claim; it is the experiment.*

**Ch. 1–4, 6 — untouched.** No verdict moves, no numbers added.

## Curator-flagged doc-hygiene (not contradictions — Hub matters)

1. **⚠ The wave's most misquotable sentence, stated in advance, is its headline.** *"`K_learned(4)` goes 16 → 128 = `K_designed(4)`"* will be read as *"the learned capacity law now equals the designed one"* by anyone skimming. It has **four guards** (**d = 4 only** · the **σ_obs / designed-arm reference** in the same sentence · **no `tol`-metric number at m > 1** · **no `K_learned` at `pscale ≠ 1`**) and all four now travel with it in the registry, the matrix, the roadmap, the primers and this ledger. **This is the w25 "0.824 → 1.000" problem one wave later, with the stakes higher because the number is now a *capacity* number rather than a mechanism one.** *Recommend the Hub check it at every draft review, and specifically at the C1-close.*
2. **⚠ A second misquotation risk that runs the other way — the one the program should be most careful to keep.** *"At matched bytes CLU beats the whole replay field"* is **true and reversible in one clause**: both launders beat it. The registry, matrix and roadmap all state the two in the **same sentence**, never in adjacent ones. ⛔ **A draft that separates them into different paragraphs is non-compliant.**
3. **⚠ One convention difference between a report and the handover — labelled, not resolved.** `r2-excursion-reach` quotes **population** sd (0.8210 ± 0.0028 → 0.9997 ± 0.0005); the Hub's §10 entry and the C1W27 task file quote the **sample** sd of the same data (± 0.0034 → ± 0.0006). The Hub flagged this itself (*"immaterial, but do not 'correct' its tables"*). **The docs carry the report's numbers with the convention named.** ⇒ **The papers must choose one convention once, program-wide** — this is exactly the class of thing a referee finds by comparing two sections.
4. **⚠ A defect fixed and a defect ruled, both moving WORDING rather than numbers.** **D1 is RESOLVED** (`evict` now scrubs `centers`/`payloads`; verified neutral two ways — `max |ΔV| = 0.0`, and the whole relocate arm reproduces its published numbers to 4–5 dp) ⇒ *"the item is gone"* is now true at the data-structure level. **D3 is RULED** (the trilemma; both fixes refuted; option (d) recommended **behind a flag, defaults OFF**) ⇒ nothing in the potential changes until the flagged build is measured, **including `R₅₀`**.
5. **⚠ The seed exposure has moved, and it now sits on the *positive*.** w25's thin flank was the Stage-0 table; w26's is the unclamping itself: **K=128 is 2 seeds**, **arm (b)'s K=64 FAIL rests on 1 seed + the 2× atom re-check**, and **the theorist's 31/32 has no error bar (1 seed per learned cell)**. The verdict margins are enormous (~110 SE at the decisive cell); the *digits* on the ladder's top rung are thin. **A 3-seed repeat at K=128 and at K=64 is cheap and would harden the table most likely to become the R2 figure.**
6. **⚠ Two "NOT RUN" items must never decay into nulls, and one of them is a Head ruling.** The **CIFAR entry** is a **pre-registered, Head-DECLINED NOT-RUN** (its expected outcome — CLU 0.30–0.36, `laundered = True` — is on the record *in advance*, which is what makes declining it defensible rather than convenient). The **d = 6 / d = 8 sweep** and **item 3 (the P4 sharding probe, killed at load 139.76)** are compute-declared NOT-RUNs. All three are filed as such in the registry and future-work register. ⛔ *A "not run" that is later cited as "no effect" is the one failure mode this wave's discipline was designed to prevent.*
7. **⚠ Chapter 8 now carries FOUR addenda over unrevised §-bodies — the recommendation I made at w25 has reached its trigger.** I said then that *"if w26's contested-win experiment lands either way, that is the natural moment for a Chapter 8 §5 restatement (append-only, superseding nothing)."* **It landed** — and two dials moved materially on top of it. **The trigger has fired; the call is the Hub's.** *As at w19–w25, the curator does not restructure a document whose structure encodes the program's beliefs — but a reader now assembles the current state of each dial from five places, and C1 closes one wave from now, which is the last cheap moment to do it.*
8. **No output↔handover numeric disagreement found this pass.** Every headline in the Hub's §10 w26 entry reproduces against the seven reports (the Hub re-derived four of them from raw JSON itself); the only difference found anywhere is the **population-vs-sample sd convention** in item 3, which the Hub had already labelled. **Nothing to adjudicate.**

# ⟲ C1W27 addendum — the closing wave (2026-07-31, doc-curator `c1w27-c2w1-sync`, **[C2W3]**, written three waves late) — **Campaign 1 ends by converting two open directions into finished statements and killing its last surviving win-route by measurement — and the honest version of that sentence is the strongest thing C1 hands to C2**

> **⚠ Provenance & scope (read first).**
> **(1) Sources.** The four C1W27 output reports (`r2-d-sweep-close`, `deletion-waitlist-stiffness`, `learned-confine-causal`, `r1-positioning-pass`), the Hub's §10 **`[C1W27]` review entry** (2026-07-30; **5/5 ACCEPTED**, integrated → `main @ e0f06d3`, **761 green**, zero conflicts — third consecutive wave) **including its own verification block**, and the **`[C1-CLOSE]`** entry. **Every number is transcribed with its source; no verdict is reinterpreted.**
> **(2) ⟲ protocol respected.** Chapters 1–8 are **not rewritten.** Nothing deleted anywhere.
> **(3) ⚠ This addendum is THREE WAVES LATE and is the wave's PRIMARY ledger record.** The C1W27 sync was owed and never written; C1 retired with it explicitly outstanding. **No Hub addendum exists for C1W27, C2W1 or C2W2** — the file ended at ⟲ Wave-26. All three below are primary records, not supplements.
